Learning path

Each declaration appears after its prerequisites.

  1. Bulgaria1998P11.exists_prime_fac_mod_three_eq_two A natural number that is 2 mod 3 has a prime factor that is 2 mod 3
  2. Bulgaria1998P11.mod_three_ne_two_of_sq_eq_neg_three Documented declaration: mod_three_ne_two_of_sq_eq_neg_three
  3. Bulgaria1998P6.sq_mod_four_of_odd The square of an odd integer is `1` modulo `4`
  4. Bulgaria1998P6.descent_aux Documented declaration: descent_aux
  5. Bulgaria1998P8.S helper function Sₙ₋₁(x,y) = (x + y)² - 1(x + 1)Pₙ₋₁(y+2, x+2)
  6. Bulgaria1998P8.T helper function Tₙ₋₁(x,y) = (y - y²)(x - x²)Pₙ₋₁(y, x)
  7. Bulgaria1998P8.U helper function Uₙ₋₁(x,y) = (x + y - 1) [(y + 1)(x - x²)Pₙ₋₁(y + 2, x) + (x + 1)(y - y²) Pₙ₋₁(y, x + 2)]
  8. CIIM2022P6.mul_le_self_pow If `p ≥ 2` and `1 ≤ v` then `v * p ≤ p ^ v`
  9. CIIM2022P6.sum_div_pow_le_one Key combinatorial estimate
  10. CIIM2022P6.prod_factorization_le The multiplicative bound `∏_{p | n+1} (v_p(n!) + v_p(n+1) + 1) ≤ 2 ∏_{p | n+1} (v_p(n!) + 1)`
  11. Imo1959P1.imo1959_p1 IMO 1959 Problem 1: an irreducible fraction
  12. Imo1959P3.formedQuadratic Documented declaration: formedQuadratic
  13. Imo1959P3.formedQuadraticEval The formed quadratic evaluated at `t`, i.e
  14. Imo1959P3.sq_b_mul_cos Squaring the rearranged equation `b * cos x = -(a * cos x ^ 2 + c)` eliminates the odd power of `cos x`
  15. Imo1959P3.formedQuadratic_cos_two_mul The formed quadratic evaluated at `cos (2 * x)`, expressed back in terms of `cos x` via `cos (2 * x) = 2 * cos x ^ 2 - 1`
  16. Imo1959P3.imo1959_p3_comparison For `a = 4`, `b = 2`, `c = -1` the formed equation in `cos 2x` is four times the original equation in `cos x`: the two equations are the same
  17. Imo1960P1.not_zero Proving that three digit numbers are the ones in [100, 1000)
  18. Imo1960P1.SearchUpTo We do an exhaustive search to show that all results are covered by `SolutionPredicate`
  19. Imo1960P1.left_direction Now we just need to prove the equivalence, for the precise problem statement
  20. Imo1960Q2.IsGood The predicate says that `x` satisfies the inequality \[\dfrac{4x^2}{(1 - \sqrt {2x + 1})^2} < 2x + 9\] and belongs to the domain of the function on the left-hand side
  21. Imo1961P2.weitzenbock_identity Helper lemma: The key algebraic identity for Weitzenböck's inequality
  22. Imo1961P2.imo1961_p2a IMO 1961 P2 (Inequality Case)**
  23. Imo1961P2.imo1961_p2b IMO 1961 P2 (Equality Case)**
  24. Imo1962P1.helper_5_digit Putting this inline causes a deep recursion error, so we separate it out
  25. Imo1962P1.case_more_digits `linarith` fails on numbers this large, so this lemma spells out some of the arithmetic that normally would be automated
  26. Imo1962P1.imo1962_p1 IMO 1962 Problem 1: moving the last digit 6 to the front
  27. Imo1962P4.altFormula The key to solving this problem simply is that we can rewrite the equation as a product of terms, shown in `alt_formula`, being equal to zero
  28. Imo1962P4.solve_cos2_half Now we can solve for `x` using basic-ish trigonometry
  29. Imo1963P6.secondPrediction The second prediction "D, A, E, C, B", given as the function sending each student to the place in which it was predicted to finish
  30. Imo1963P6.classification The conditions of the problem determine the outcome uniquely: there are only `5!
  31. Imo1964P1.solution_set
  32. Imo1964P1.imo1964_p1a IMO 1964 Problem 1(a): when 7 divides 2^n - 1 after Imo1964P1.solution_set
  33. Imo1964P1.imo_1964_p1b' An alternative proof, heavily golfed
  34. Imo1964P2.schur TODO: get this into mathlib in some form
  35. Imo1964P4.lemma1 Smaller version of the problem, with 6 (or more) people and 2 topics
  36. Imo1967P6.MedalsProcess The process described in the problem, from the point of view of the medals remaining: `r k` is the number of medals left at the start of day `k + 1` (so `r 0 = m`)
  37. Imo1967P6.rec_eq The day-`k` rule implies the recurrence `7 * r k = 6 * (r (k - 1) - k)`
  38. Imo1967P6.closed_form Solving the recurrence: after `k` days the number of remaining medals is `(6/7)^k * (m - 36) + 36 - 6k`
  39. Imo1967P6.key_eq Specializing the closed form at `k = n - 1` (where `r (n - 1) = n`) gives the key Diophantine relation between `m` and `n`
  40. Imo1967P6.forward The forward direction: the Diophantine relation forces `n = 6` and `m = 36`, because `6 ^ (n - 1)` must divide `n - 6` while growing much faster
  41. Imo1967P6.backward The converse direction: with `m = 36` and `n = 6` exactly six medals are awarded on each of the six days
  42. Imo1968P6.term_telescope Lemma for the telescoping term structure
  43. Imo1968P6.n_ans The answer is n
  44. Imo1969P1.goodNats `goodNats` is the set of natural numbers satisfying the condition in the problem statement, namely the `a : ℕ` such that `n^4 + a` is not prime for any `n : ℕ`
  45. Imo1969P1.factorization The key to the solution is that you can factor $z$ into the product of two polynomials, if $a = 4*m^4$
  46. Imo1969P1.polynomial_not_prime Every natural number of the form `n^4 + 4*m^4` is not prime
  47. Imo1969P1.aChoice We define $a_{choice}(b) := 4*(2+b)^4$, so that we can take $m = 2+b$ in `polynomial_not_prime`
  48. Imo1969P1.aChoice_strictMono `aChoice` is a strictly monotone function; this is easily proven by chaining together lemmas in the `strictMono` namespace
  49. Imo1969P6.amgm_chain Documented declaration: amgm_chain
  50. Imo1969P6.eqCondition The condition for equality to hold

  52. Imo1970P2.pow_cross_lt For `i < n` and `b < a` we have `bⁿ aⁱ < aⁿ bⁱ`
  53. Imo1970P2.value_succ Splitting off the leading digit: `value x c n = value x c (n-1) + x n * cⁿ`
  54. Imo1970P2.value_pos The full number is positive since its leading digit is nonzero
  55. Imo1970P2.pow_mul_value_lt The crux: `bⁿ · B' < aⁿ · A'`, where `A'` and `B'` are the numbers obtained by stripping the leading digit
  56. Imo1970P2.value_mul_lt Cross-multiplied form of the inequality: `A' · B < B' · A`

  70. Imo1970P6.four_point Among four points in the plane, no three of which are collinear, some triple forms a triangle with an angle of at least `π / 2`
  71. Imo1970P6.AcuteSet The predicate from the problem statement: the 3-element subset `w` of indices spans an acute-angled triangle
  72. Imo1970P6.exists_nonacute_triple Among any four of the points, some three of them form a non-acute triangle
  73. Imo1970P6.card_supersets The number of `(w.card + k)`-element subsets of `s` containing a fixed `w ⊆ s` is `(s.card - w.card).choose k`
  74. Imo1970P6.five_point_bound Among any five of the points, at least three of the ten 3-element subsets form a non-acute triangle
  75. Imo1970P6.global_bound Globally, at least `3 * (100.choose 5) / (97.choose 2) = 48510` of the 3-element subsets form a non-acute triangle
  76. Imo1971P1.prod_ne_eq_prod_ite This makes working with the sum easier and simp more powerful

  122. Imo1976P3.floor_spec The defining property of `⌊n / k⌋₊`, expressed with integers only: `2 * n'^3 ≤ n^3 < 2 * (n' + 1)^3`, obtained by cubing `n' * k ≤ n < (n' + 1) * k`

  132. Imo1976P5.valueFinset The candidate values for the tuple of unknowns: integers between `-n` and `n` in each coordinate

  137. Imo1977P3.Vn The set `Vₙ` of natural numbers of the form `1 + k * n` with `k` a positive integer
  138. Imo1977P3.Indecomposable `m` is indecomposable in `Vₙ` if it belongs to `Vₙ` but cannot be written as a product of two members of `Vₙ`
  139. Imo1977P3.vn_ge Every member of `Vₙ` is at least `n + 1`
  140. Imo1977P3.mul_mem_vn `Vₙ` is closed under multiplication
  141. Imo1977P3.indecomposable_sq `(n - 1)^2` (written with `n = c + 3`) is indecomposable in `Vₙ`: it is smaller than the square of the smallest member of `Vₙ`
  142. Imo1977P3.not_dvd `(n - 1)^2` does not divide `(n - 1) * (2 * n - 1)` (written with `n = c + 3`), since `n - 1` does not divide `2 * n - 1 = 2 * (n - 1) + 1`
  143. Imo1977P3.exists_factorization Every member of `Vₙ` is a product of indecomposable members of `Vₙ`
  144. Imo1977P5.sq_add_sq_eq 1009 is prime and `≡ 1 (mod 4)`, so its representation as a sum of two squares is unique: `1009 = 15² + 28²`
  145. Imo1978P3.a The lower Wythoff sequence `⌊n·φ⌋`, which is the function `f` in the problem

  153. Imo1979P2.eq_of_ne_of_ne In a two-element palette, any two elements that both differ from a third element are equal

  483. Imo2000P4.case_ends Case 2b of the induction step: the restriction to `1, ..., n` is an `Ends` placement with `4 ≤ n`; then there is no room for the card `n + 1`

  655. Imo2012P3.aliceWeight_le The total weight never exceeds `1000 * N`

  657. Imo2012P3.alice_lie_streak If A's `i`-th answer is a lie for `x`, then `x`'s streak increases by one

  660. Imo2012P3.not_bobWins Under the weight bound, B has no winning strategy: A's counterplay is consistent with every candidate at all times, so B can never stop with a correct guess of size at most `n`

  662. Imo2012P3.exists_eventually_large For all sufficiently large `k`, `1000 * ((1.99) ^ k + 2) < (1.998) ^ (k + 1)`

  2581. Usa2014P4.ACanWin_five `A` wins for `k = 5`, following the official solution: after two rounds a pair of adjacent counters remains, from which the strategy above wins

  2583. Usa2014P6.sum_range_one_div_le_aux Dyadic bound for harmonic sums: the sum of `1/(k+1)` for `k < 2^m - 1` is at most `m`
  2584. Usa2014P6.sum_Icc_one_div_le_clog The harmonic sum `∑ k ∈ Icc 1 M, 1/k` is at most `Nat.clog 2 (M + 1)`

  2586. Usa2014P6.card_filter_dvd_le The number of `i < N` with `p ∣ a + i` is at most `N / p + 1`, as a rational inequality

  2591. Usa2015P3.Coloring A coloring of the subsets of `Fin n`; `true` means blue, `false` means red
  2592. Usa2015P3.blueCard `blueCard c T` is the number of subsets of `T` that are colored blue by `c`; this is the function `f` of the problem statement

  2599. Usa2015P3.blueCard_ne_zero_X The intersection `X c` of all blue sets contains a blue subset
  2600. Usa2015P3.X_blue The intersection `X c` of all blue sets is itself blue
  2601. Usa2015P3.blueCard_X `X c` is the only blue subset of itself
  2602. Usa2015P3.blueCard_union Multiplicativity of `blueCard` over pairs of sets whose intersection is `X c`
  2603. Usa2015P3.blueCard_singleton `X c ∪ {i}` has one blue subset if it is red (`X c` itself) and two if it is blue
  2604. Usa2015P3.blueCard_prod `blueCard` is multiplicative over the elements of a set disjoint from `X c`
  2605. Usa2015P3.blueCard_pow For `U` disjoint from `X c`, the number of blue subsets of `X c ∪ U` is a power of two determined by which of the `X c ∪ {i}` are blue
  2606. Usa2015P3.blue_of_mem_interval Every set in the interval `[X c, X c ∪ B c]` is blue
  2607. Usa2015P3.interval_of_blue Every blue set lies in the interval `[X c, X c ∪ B c]`

  2609. Usa2015P3.colorOf The coloring whose blue sets are exactly the sets `T` with `X₀ ⊆ T ⊆ X₀ ∪ B₀`
  2610. Usa2015P3.DisjointPairs The type of disjoint pairs `(X₀, B₀)` of finsets of `Fin n`; these parametrize the nontrivial valid colorings
  2611. Usa2015P3.X_colorOf The blue interval `[X₀, X₀ ∪ B₀]` of a disjoint pair determines a valid coloring, and conversely every nontrivial valid coloring arises this way
  2612. Usa2015P3.fromColoring The map from a valid coloring to its parametrizing data: `none` for the all-red coloring, and the disjoint pair `(X c, B c)` otherwise

  2623. Usa2015P4.EquivPilings.snd The multiset of columns is invariant under equivalence of pilings

  2984. Usa2020P6.sum_perm_apply₂ Summing `g (σ i) (σ j)` over all permutations, for `i ≠ j`

  2986. Usa2020P6.sum_S_sq The second moment `∑ σ, (S σ)² = (n-1)!

  2988. Usa2020P6.fin_strictMono_ge A strictly monotone function `Fin m → ℕ` dominates the identity

  2991. Usa2020P6.bottomsum The sum of an antitone function over any finset is at least the sum over a terminal segment of the same cardinality
  2992. Usa2020P6.abel_nonneg Summation by parts: if `z` has zero sum and nonnegative partial sums, and `x` is decreasing on `range n`, then `∑ x i * z i ≥ 0`
  2993. Usa2020P6.rearr_upper Rearrangement inequality (upper bound): for two decreasing sequences, the "identity" pairing maximizes `∑ x i * y (σ i)` over all permutations `σ`
  2994. Usa2020P6.rearr_lower Rearrangement inequality (lower bound): for two decreasing sequences, the "reversed" pairing minimizes `∑ x i * y (σ i)` over all permutations `σ`
